Like, I don’t really do anything specific after. I mostly just feel bad about it and try to tell myself I’ll eat more normally next time, if that counts. But I’m not, like… doing anything on purpose to undo it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t make myself throw up after. I mean, I’ve felt really guilty or uncomfortable, but I haven’t done that. Is that… is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I do. I try not to make a huge deal out of it, but afterward I feel pretty embarrassed, and kind of… grossed out with myself, I guess. Especially because in other parts of my life, like school or robotics, I’m usually really disciplined, so it makes me feel like I’m not in control. Is that the kind of guilt you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any big changes in your weight over time because of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t noticed anything huge, or at least I don’t think so. My weight kind of goes up and down a little like normal, but I haven’t been tracking it closely. I guess if you think I should pay more attention to that, I can.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s been happening most weeks, since pretty early in the semester. Usually it’s just one night, not like every day or anything… sometimes two if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food sitting around the apartment. It’s probably been like that for the last three or four months, I think. Does that sound like the kind of frequency you’re asking about?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I might think, like, “Okay, I should eat lighter tomorrow,” but I don’t really go a whole day without eating or anything extreme like that. At least, I haven’t noticed myself doing that after. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Then I look down and it’s just… gone in a few minutes. I don’t know if that counts as more quickly than usual, but it probably is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes where you feel like you've lost control of your eating are typically followed by any other behaviors besides just feeling guilty or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I don’t think there’s anything specific I do after, besides feeling gross about it or trying to just move on. Like I might avoid thinking about it or tell myself I’ll be more normal tomorrow, but I don’t think there’s some other behavior I’m doing. If there’s something in particular you mean, I can try to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like fasting on purpose. I might think, like, “Okay, I should eat lighter tomorrow,” but I don’t really go a whole day without eating or anything extreme like that. At least, I haven’t noticed myself doing that after. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to eat less or follow a very strict diet after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might have the thought that I should be “better” or maybe not snack as much the next day, but I don’t actually do some strict diet thing. But the next morning I can feel kind of sluggish or distracted, and I’ll think about it more than I want to.\n\nAnd honestly, I sometimes worry about whether my roommates noticed, which feels embarrassing to say. Like, I don’t really do anything specific after. I mostly just feel bad about it and try to tell myself I’ll eat more normally next time, if that counts. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but once I start eating like that, it kind of feels like a switch flips, and I just keep going even though part of me is thinking, “Dennis, just stop already.” Like I know I should be able to stop, but in that moment it doesn’t really feel like I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise more than usual after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… not really, I don’t think. I mean, I might feel kind of like I should do something healthier the next day, but I don’t usually go work out extra or anything like that. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever use laxatives or diuretics after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I know what laxatives are, but I haven’t used them after eating like that. Diuretics… those are like water pills, right? No, not something I do. I’d tell you if I was, I think.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever eaten a large amount of food in a short period of time without feeling like you lost control of your eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess there are times I eat a lot where it’s not exactly that out-of-control feeling. Like after a project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I might take it back to my room and end up having a whole small pizza, and then chips or cereal too. It’s definitely more than I planned, and probably more than my roommates would eat for dinner, but… I don’t know, sometimes it feels more like I’m just letting myself keep going, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you're having one of these eating episodes, do you feel like you're eating more quickly than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not every single time, but during those times I kind of eat fast. Like I’ll be standing at my desk between homework and a game or something, and I’m not really paying attention to tasting it. Then I look down and it’s just… gone in a few minutes. I don’t know if that counts as more quickly than usual, but it probably is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these episodes where you feel like you've lost control of your eating are typically followed by any other behaviors besides just feeling guilty or uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure. I don’t think there’s anything specific I do after, besides feeling gross about it or trying to just move on. Like I might avoid thinking about it or tell myself I’ll be more normal tomorrow, but I don’t think there’s some other behavior I’m doing. I might think, like, “Okay, I should eat lighter tomorrow,” but I don’t really go a whole day without eating or anything extreme like that. At least, I haven’t noticed myself doing that after. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to eat less or follow a very strict diet after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might have the thought that I should be “better” or maybe not snack as much the next day, but I don’t actually do some strict diet thing. I usually just try to eat normally and not make a big deal out of it, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you're having one of these eating episodes, do you think you're consuming a lot more calories than you normally would in a similar amount of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like a whole small pizza, and then chips, and sometimes cereal too, just in my room. That’s definitely more than I’d normally eat for dinner, and probably more than my roommates would eat in that same time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever do anything else to try to make up for the calories you consumed during one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so.
